'zero tolerance', mrs stauner, is not to say that we no longer carry out checks, but to draw the appropriate consequences if something is found. mr van dam, i should like to say briefly the following about your remarks: the directorate-general for budget is not the directorate-general for financial control. i'll say it again. we have now decentralized financial control, and in all their home countries it is the same, namely that the ministry responsible for a particular programme is always responsible for control and for the correct implementation, and cannot hide behind another directorate-general. that was the mistake in the past. the internal audit service is an independent service and i believe that it has indeed demonstrated this during the years in which mr muis was in charge of the internal audit service. i would like to return to the subject of live transport, export subsidies and exports of live cattle. it's just that this is allowed. the council decision also provides for subsidies to live exports, albeit at a lower level than in the past. i cannot therefore say that the fact that live cattle are exported is fraudulent. it's legal. i share your concern to ask in this area: what is ethically acceptable from the point of view of animal welfare? i mentioned that the commission has tightened up the rules, and we have seen this in the case of egypt. if the european union then stops exporting, if these transports are instead made from australia, then nothing is gained in terms of animal welfare. it is therefore important to act in the interests of animal welfare, not only internally, but also to find allies. i take note of your critical remarks in this area and will pass them on to my colleague mr fischler, and i will ask him to provide you with further information if you wish. i shall certainly teach him the critical tenor on this subject in this house.

commission. mr president, may i again thank the rapporteur, mrs keppelhoff-wiechert, and all of those involved in taking this important dossier forward. based on the excellent work done and the informal trialogue between parliament, council and commission, there is, i hope, sufficient agreement between the three institutions to reach resolution in first reading. i understand the view expressed in the house in favour of financial guarantees. however, i have also to take into account the view of the council, where there is a qualified majority in favour of deleting the provision on guarantees. it is clear to me that if first reading agreement is to be reached, this house will need to support the amendment to delete the guarantees in line with the compromise proposed. the commission could then come forward with a report on all aspects of the situation. this would enable full consultation of all economic actors and stakeholders. i have to say that a very persuasive factor is the fact that the insurance industry across the community, including the enlarged community, is not ready for mandatory financial guarantees across the board. there is little point in legislating if we cannot be assured of effective compliance. so i would strongly urge the house to support the amendment in relation to guarantees; otherwise a golden opportunity for first reading agreement will be lost. based on the compromise reached, i understand that the rapporteur has tabled amendments which the commission can accept and thereby facilitate closure in first reading. finally, i would like to state that a full listing of the commission's position on the amendments is being provided to parliament. i trust that this will be included in the verbatim report of proceedings of this part-session commission's position on amendments by parliament: see annex..

- the next item is the report (a5-0147/2004) by mr thors, on behalf of the committee on the environment, public health and consumer policy, on the proposal for a regulation of the european parliament and of the council on materials and articles intended to come into contact with foodstuffs.
the commission. mr president, ladies and gentlemen, i would like to thank you for your support. first of all, i would like to thank parliament for its response to the commission's communication on a european strategy for the environment and health. i would like to thank in particular the two rapporteurs, first frédérique ries and then marit paulsen, for their work, and i can certainly extend this to the whole period that marit paulsen has been in the european parliament and the remarkably good work she has done in this particular area. environment and health are issues that concern us all. according to the latest eurobarometer surveys, 89% of europeans are concerned about the impact of the environment on their health. nearly 10 percent report having a close relative or family member who suffers from an environmental illness. our knowledge of what in the environment actually causes certain health effects is still quite limited. disease is caused by many factors, often interacting. how much the environment contributes to the diseases we do not know with absolute certainty today. one of the main objectives of this strategy is to increase our knowledge and thus to provide a better basis for tackling the problems. the idea is to bring together environment, health and research to better understand and thereby tackle more effectively the complex relationship between environment and health. i do not want to underestimate that this is also a challenge, because in our traditional roles we are not always used to working horizontally across the traditional boundaries between different research disciplines and policy areas. we are currently working on an action plan for the period 2004 to 2010. we will present it at the meeting of environment and health ministers in budapest in june 2004, which is a pan-european meeting. the preparation of this action plan has involved experts and stakeholders from across the enlarged eu. we have had regional conferences in warsaw, rome and brussels, where many of the issues raised by parliament in its report have been discussed. it is important to remember that the strategy is long-term. our knowledge of the role of the environment in various health problems will not be complete immediately. expresses concern that the strategy focuses only on information gathering and research; in a sense, yes, but we need this information to know how best to direct our efforts. that doesn't mean we're going to sit idly by. in some areas we cannot risk waiting until we have full knowledge, but then we will act on the precautionary principle, and we will. for the first phase, covering the period 2004-2010, we have selected a number of diseases and priority topics to be addressed in particular: respiratory diseases in children, asthma and allergies, neurodevelopmental disorders, childhood cancers, endocrine disrupting effects or endocrine disrupting effects. it is clear that children are a priority target group and are also the most exposed to environmental risks, so we have put them at the centre of our strategy. the selected substances are heavy metals, dioxins, pcbs and endocrine disruptors. based on these diseases and substances, we want to build a programme that can provide us with the necessary information on how the environment affects our health. we hope that we can then extend it to other environmental threats and linked diseases. in the report, parliament points to the risk of duplication of work, that we should not take into account existing research results or work carried out, for example, by the world health organisation. we are very aware of this risk and the coordination of information from different areas is one of our most important tasks. i would like to emphasize that from the very beginning of the work on this environmental and health strategy we have cooperated with and sought the views of the world health organization. in conclusion, i would like to thank parliament once again for its support on this very important issue which, i assume, is close to the hearts of all of us. together with comments from other institutions and from interest groups, this report, and not least the work of marit paulsen, will help us to develop an effective and good action plan. thank you for your attention.
mr president, the link between the environment and health was a request from parliament, based in particular on the requirements of the treaties, articles 152 and 174. i would remind you that in 1996 the formulation of the protection of public health as a separate theme in the then-forming fifth environment and sustainable development programme was my proposal which was accepted by all the institutions. for the first time, the european union was attempting to collect and disseminate information and knowledge on the effects of the state of the environment on human health. today, the sixth environment programme includes this specific theme, namely environment - health - quality of life, while the first community action programme in the field of public health, which we adopted in 2002 and for which i had the honour of being rapporteur, contains the promotion of the recording, analysis and evaluation of the determinants of health linked to the environment. this knowledge will obviously lead to the adoption and promotion of appropriate strategies and measures to protect human health from the adverse effects of a degraded and dangerous environment. it should be noted, however, that the union has been reluctant to take environmental health into account up to now, and therefore the present proposal for a strategy on environment and health can only be welcomed as a positive step reflecting the european union's willingness to take action in this area, because the environmental risks to health are now high and require a modern and integrated approach. emphasis should be placed on reducing environmental risks that affect human health or cause disease, and this at great economic and social cost. of course, special attention should be paid to the most vulnerable groups of the population, children, the elderly, pregnant women, and people with a serious medical history. the action programme proposes measures to improve air quality in the living and working environment, studies to address the most serious emissions and exposure, special attention to populations living near sources of pollution and a reinforced preventive policy focused on intensifying environmental monitoring of carcinogens, reproductive and nervous system toxicants. it also stressed the importance of environmental education in order to take initiatives to raise public awareness of the need for information. mr president, it is true that the environment, together with nutrition, is a major determinant of health and that the union's strategy in this field must therefore include the continuous collection and systematic monitoring of data linking environmental parameters to various diseases. only by continuously developing scientific knowledge and research on aspects of certain diseases with environmental parameters will we be able to develop effectively the union's strategy and take action to protect human health. this strategy will make it possible to further increase the life expectancy of european citizens.
mr president, commissioner, the commission communication on the european strategy for the environment and health is an ambitious step towards improving the quality of life of european citizens. there is now clear evidence that 20% of the total number of diseases in industrialized countries are related to environmental factors. most of these diseases affect children, women of reproductive age, the poor and vulnerable groups. in the fifteen member states 60 000 deaths each year are due to long-term exposure to particulate matter. 10% of children in western europe have asthma, ten times the rate of children in eastern europe, and this is due to the different environment. a 10% reduction in ozone is expected to cause 300 000 skin cancers and 4500 cases of melanoma annually worldwide. the community's action policies to date have concentrated on specific environmental and health-related factors, but the risks from the combination and interaction of the various pollutants in their entirety on the human body have never been assessed. i believe that the proposed strategy helps to provide a comprehensive understanding of the threats, how they affect human health and what action is required. the scale initiative, with its gradual and cyclical implementation, is based on scientific research to obtain the necessary information, focuses on children as the most vulnerable population group, increases awareness that environmental degradation is harmful to the health of all and in particular of children, uses the legal means provided by the treaty to take relevant action and, finally, provides for continuous and continuous evaluation of the effectiveness of actions. on behalf of my group, i would like to say that we support the commission's communication as it stands, but we will not support amendments which depart from the spirit of globality and concentrate on a few areas to the exclusion of others, which are equally important.
in writing. - (el) the the resolution recognises what the kke has also condemned, namely that the eu, which has appointed itself as the guardian of fundamental rights, allows a serious breach of privacy by transferring sensitive personal data from eu airlines to the us. it also points out that the commission and the national authorities acted unlawfully by failing to initiate legal proceedings to prevent the airlines from disclosing that information. the commission is also asked to withdraw the draft resolution it submitted a few months ago after negotiations with the united states, in which the eu actually complies with the united states' demands. however, the conclusions of the motion for a resolution do not propose a ban on any transfer of personal data, but rather assess the existence of a legislative gap and propose an agreement with the united states merely with less data and shorter data retention, and where the data subjects are given the right to have the data recorded rectified. for the immediate future, it is proposed that passengers should give their consent to the disclosure of 39 (!) pieces of information. any refusal to do so will, of course, mean that you will not be allowed to enter the united states. whatever the intentions of the authors, it is once again clear that the european parliament is accepting the terms of the united states as a second deputy sheriff. for these reasons, the kke members of the european parliament have neither voted for nor against.
the rapporteur. mr president, as is well known, croatia submitted on 21 february 2003 an application for accession to the european union, with all the formalities and responsibility required for such action, on which the commission will express its opinion in april. the european parliament, through my report, has prepared, in accordance with rule 49 (1), a proposal for a recommendation to the council on croatia's application for membership, which was voted in favour in the committee on foreign affairs. the report was carefully balanced and well documented, so that the european parliament's initiative to recommend to the council would be taken into account and represent the views and demands of all parties in an exemplary manner. i thank my colleagues for their help in this direction. in summary, the main points of this report, to which croatia should give due attention, are the following: first, the international criminal tribunal for the former yugoslavia. the council also noted the progress made towards cooperation with the international criminal court and the outstanding issues, in particular the case of general kotovina before the court. secondly, the return of refugees and displaced persons. parliament attaches great importance to the fulfilment of this obligation, which it considers to be one of the main criteria for croatia's accession to the european union. it welcomes the election of minority mps to parliament, but expects more in ensuring all the conditions for the return of refugees and displaced persons, such as legal protection, housing, reconciliation and their smooth integration into croatian society, without discrimination and fear. thirdly, good neighbourly regional cooperation. good neighbourliness and regional cooperation are on the right track and the report stresses that this is a key prerequisite for the development of democracy in croatia itself, but also a model for ensuring peace, stability and economic development in the region as a whole. fourthly, the judicial system and the administrative infrastructure. the revision and adaptation of the judicial system in line with the european union's requirements is noted, but exemplary application and undoubted social acceptance in the interpretation of laws and the enhancement of the status of judges are expected. the modern administrative infrastructure is also a prerequisite for the proper management of community resources, among other things, and particular attention is needed to combat any instances of corruption and to provide citizens with a rapid service. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. sixth, freedom of expression and independence of the media. freedom of expression and the independence of the media are essential conditions for the functioning of democracy and no interference by state bodies in the exercise of freedom of expression can be justified. seventh, the free market economy. it recognises croatia's progress in the functioning of the free market and points to croatia's successes in both annual gross national product growth in recent years and in reducing the level of inflation, which is around the european union average. the commission has already made a number of proposals to this effect. the new government's determination to continue the previous government's efforts towards european orientation is welcomed and the new government's readiness to promote all the necessary reforms to enable croatia to move quickly towards the objective of european union accession, which it wishes, is particularly appreciated. we call on the house to vote in favour of this report, together with the amendment tabled by the socialist group, which i support. i would like to thank the commissioner for his reply.
mr president, the annual report on fundamental rights comes at a difficult time, marked by the recent terrorist attack in spain and the violation of the right to life of dozens of innocent people. the european council has unfortunately concentrated on the outward appearance rather than on the substance and causes of the new situation created by the war on democracy, as president pat cox called it. it is certain that the repressive measures being promoted not only fail to address the root of the evil, which is mainly the enormous inequalities and discriminations of the modern world, but threaten to erode fundamental freedoms and democratic rights, thus further strengthening the objectives of terrorism. so we're being led with mathematical precision into an orwellian situation where privacy will be nonexistent and privacy will be permanently under the scrutiny of big brother. terrorism is the excuse for the inflation of security spending and the transformation of modern democracies into authoritarian police states where every citizen is treated as a potential terrorist. and while all of this is happening on the front of terrorism, another horrific crime committed daily against thousands of women and children, in the form of modern day slavery, is at the very bottom of our political agenda. the european union is facing a serious threat to fundamental rights and democratic freedoms.

(parliament adopted the legislative resolution) the report gives a fairly credible picture of the european union, which is very different from the make-up profile of a model in respect for human rights so often presented by the european union's propagandists. it contains several critical references to the situation of fundamental rights in the eu, the treatment of prisoners, slavery and forced labour, the protection of personal data, freedom of expression and access to information, the right to asylum, racism and xenophobia, gender discrimination, disabled people, labour rights, the rights of third country nationals and fair access to justice. however, this self-criticism is undermined because the report considers it a criterion that the eu charter of fundamental rights is respected, even though it does not correspond at all to the content of several constitutions, including the greek one. our most basic objection, however, concerns the treatment of fundamental rights such as the right to work, free health care, schooling, welfare, etc. at a time when capital is attacking the rights of workers head-on, it is an illusion at best to think that the problem can be solved not by fighting but by new eu directives and measures or by implementing the existing ones. as regards women's rights, the report ignores the fact that equality in social and family life can only be achieved if the capitalist core of eu policy is removed. the kke members of the european parliament therefore vote neither for nor against.
- the next item is the report (a5-0253/2004) by mr ghilardotti, on behalf of the committee on legal affairs and the internal market, on the communication from the commission to the council and the european parliament on the modernisation of company law and improved corporate governance in the european union - the way forward (com (2003) 284 - c5-0378/2003 - 2003/2150 (ini)).
the committee on employment and social affairs has adopted an opinion on the proposal for a council directive on the coordination of laws, regulations and administrative provisions relating to the taking up and pursuit of the business of credit institutions. mr president, successful corporate governance involves reducing the hedges on cross-border investment and increasing capital mobility. the european community of 25 must therefore give the business through which its international influence passes a global identity. this identity for europe cannot be dissociated from practices that are sensitive to social and environmental issues. i therefore regret that my colleagues in the european people's party have rejected my amendment in the committee on legal affairs, in which i argued that all directives on company law should include the obligation to inform and consult employees' representatives in cases where important decisions are to be taken which affect the continuity of companies and jobs. it is regrettable that whenever we try to integrate the productive system and the interests of labour, a coalition is formed against such proposals as if they were a mortal danger. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial interests are protected. but we must - and i am glad that the commissioner has accepted it - accept the distinction between large and small shareholders in any case, particularly as regards the use of modern technology in exercising shareholders' voting rights, since small shareholders are more exposed to risks. i am very pleased that the committee on legal affairs and the internal market has accepted the amendment. finally, for the same reasons, particular care should be taken to address methods that lead to the misappropriation of shareholder votes, such as arrangements that allow the informal organisation of systems for the representation of shareholders by large anonymous groups. i would like to conclude by thanking the rapporteur, with whom i agree on her basic points.
commission. mr president, there is just one point i should like to stress at the end of this debate: the matter of stakeholders and corporate social responsibility. the commission's action plan is part of an overall strategy to foster the efficiency and competitiveness of businesses and to strengthen shareholders' rights and the protection of third parties. however, there are also related initiatives which are part of that overall strategy. they include the commission's activities on corporate social responsibility and in particular the work of the european multi-stakeholder forum, set up at the initiative of my former colleague, mrs diamantopoulou. it would therefore have been inappropriate to introduce under this action plan another policy framework concerning corporate social responsibility. what matters more is to do what mrs berès has asked the commission to do, which is to take due and full account of the interests of all stakeholders in the measures the commission will adopt. the action plan clearly recognises this need and the commission is therefore committed to acting accordingly.

in writing. the president of the parliament in an interview with the cypriot newspaper φιλελεύθερος reportedly made the following comment about the cypriot leaders: "so, personally, i'm disappointed in what i think is a failure in the areas of imagination and courageous leadership that is required to be displayed at this time". the european union is also calling on the cypriot authorities to take the necessary steps to ensure that the eu's citizens are able to access the eu's information and to make use of it. i consider it unacceptable and unacceptable that the president of the european parliament, even on a personal level, is throwing all his weight into adding to the unbearable pressure, blackmail and threats that the cypriot people are receiving from the un secretary-general and his entourage, from the us government, from the european commission and the council, from turkey and many others, including political forces in greece. his intervention is an insult and, if i may say so, a disrespect to the political forces of cyprus and to the entire cypriot people, who do not need lessons in good behaviour from the president of parliament or anyone else. moreover, his comment contradicts what he said at the beginning of the same interview, that: "the choice of the communities of cyprus in the two referendums is the choice of free and sovereign peoples". does the president and others think that the only free choice the cypriot people have in the referendum is 'yes'?
in writing. mr president, a few weeks after the assassination of hamas leader sheikh yassin, the sharon government assassinated the new leader of al-radisi, proving that it not only does not want the peace process to move forward and an independent palestinian state to exist, but also adds fuel to the fire and seeks tension. the eu, as has been shown by its attitude so far, is essentially aligning itself with the us, limiting itself to verbal protests against such killings and israeli raids, while at the same time speaking - and through solana - of israel's right to self-defence and examining sharon's policy of evacuating gaza. it puts the perpetrator in the position of the victim. the kke deputies denounce the crimes of the sharon government and the hypocritical, in effect complicit, policy of the eu. we express our active solidarity with the palestinian people and call on the european parliament to demand that the eu stop arming israel and freeze the association agreement with it. mr president, today, at your invitation, the european parliament again observed a minute's silence for the young italian paramilitary executed in iraq, but it never asked for the same for the thousands of victims of the attackers in afghanistan, iraq and before in yugoslavia, as the kke members have repeatedly asked you to do. can you tell us when you intend to do so?
in writing. mr president, i have asked to speak to inform the house that the pakistani authorities have allowed the crew of the tasman spirit to return to their homes in greece and the philippines. after months of efforts by the greek government with the then foreign minister george papandreou, which have continued to this day, and the provision of guarantees by the greek government, after the reaction of our parliament, which adopted a resolution on the matter, after the activity of the troika, commissioner chris patten and the transition on the spot, the desired result has been achieved. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos at work is a very good one, and i would like to thank the rapporteur for the work he has done.
in writing. i would like to draw the house's attention to and request your immediate intervention on a very important matter concerning the survival of greek farmers who are really on the verge of disaster. the severe weather that hit greece a few months ago caused widespread damage to agricultural capital (plant, pot, etc.) throughout the country, creating serious problems for the survival of the affected farmers, who are threatened with bankruptcy if concrete measures are not taken immediately. i ask you, mr president, to intervene in order to speed up the time-consuming community approval procedures for the payment of compensation and financial aid to greek farmers, as was done in france, spain and malta, so that bankruptcy is prevented and the farmers affected can continue their productive activity.

- the next item is the report (a5-0257/2004) by mr sterckx, on behalf of the committee on safety at sea, on safety at sea.
mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, i was part of my party's mission to galicia immediately after the accident and i remember how shocked we were by the spectacle on the beaches and by the despair of the people in the area. i would therefore like to express my surprise at the blunt statement made by the representative of the spanish government at the time that in the event of an accident like that of the prestige, he would still decide to remove the ship from the coast. i would also like to stress the findings of the report on the treatment of the captain of the prestige and the fact that such treatment was later applied in pakistan to the seamen of the tasman spirit, a case on which our parliament has passed a resolution and in which these seamen have already been allowed to return home. pakistan is ahead of any other member state of the european union in this respect! i would also like to denounce a forgery, not a mistake, an obvious deliberate distortion made in the translation into greek - into greek only - of the discussion with captain maguire referred to in the report, where the translator, by adding a phrase that does not exist in mr sterckx's text, presents the captain as supporting exactly the opposite of what he actually supported and as agreeing with the actions of the spanish authorities. i would remind you that the prestige disaster and the composition and functioning of the mare committee have been the subject of considerable attention in the greek media, which of course draw their information from greek texts. it seems that the translator wanted to avoid offending the spanish government and its greek supporters. i ask everyone in charge to look into my complaint, which is mentioned in the last paragraph of the chapter entitled 'exchange of views with the captain' in the explanatory statement, and to put an end to the translation mafia in this parliament. in conclusion, i would like to express my faith in a full, i would say, cooperation with the imo.
the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the safety of shipping is not compromised by the fact that the shipping industry is not able to comply with the safety standards. what is usually happening is that industry is being blamed, seafarers are being blamed, states tend to shift all the responsibility off themselves and it is obvious that this is not true. i wish it was that simple, because we would have solved the problem. the problem of safety of navigation is obviously not the greek captain, mr maghouras, who should be treated better by the spanish authorities, as the sterckx report rightly points out, nor is it solely the problem of the single-hull tankers. it is also the maintenance of ships, as the recent sinking of the double-hulled tanker rocknes off norway shows, it is that we have no ports of refuge, in breach of community law, it is that we do not carry out port checks properly, in breach of community law again. it is a total, let me say, failure of the member states to apply the generally good legislative framework that we have in the european union. the first thing we have to do is to implement community legislation and then to look at the measures proposed in the sterckx report, to select some basic priorities and to take additional measures, such as the creation of a european coastguard. i would also point out that, obviously, because shipping is an international problem, international measures are needed and that is why cooperation with the international maritime organisation is always necessary.
the problem to be tackled, mr president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, is the violation of the existing provisions on working conditions in the freight transport sector, and our aim is of course to suppress and drastically reduce these violations. we all agreed in the committee on transport that the european commission's proposal is generally positive, but there is room for improvement. the rapporteur has handled the matter very well, showing how close he is to the transport workers. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the road safety of the road users is not compromised. the combination of this sensitivity with a sense of proportion requires that the proposed measures be treated realistically and without exaggeration. we believe that the volume of controls imposed should be commensurate with the volume of expected results. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to the full. we have also taken into account the need to harmonize as far as possible the social provisions in the field of road transport in the member states, the possibilities offered by modern technology in the field of controls, the statistical data at our disposal, and the view already expressed by the european parliament on the better implementation of the social provisions in force, as well as the views of the social partners. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. i would like to congratulate the european commission, mrs de palacio, and our rapporteur, mr markov, on their work.
in writing. - (el) i hope that the proposal for a directive on consumer credit which we are discussing will provide a solution which is beneficial to consumers and put an end to this unacceptable situation which is an eldorado for credit institutions. in greece, consumer credit has been growing rapidly in recent years and there is already talk of excessive debt on the part of greek households. this increase is due not only to misleading advertising to attract customers, but also to the opaque terms and conditions and the "small print" in the contracts, which place additional burdens on borrowers. no one can deny the need to establish measures to standardise the information that banks must provide, so that consumers can more easily compare and choose the best product for their needs. this information should include the actual annual interest rate, any costs of entering into the contract, the number and amount of instalments and the total cost of the loan. it is also necessary to introduce the right of the consumer to withdraw from the contract within a reasonable period of time without having to justify his action.
in the light of the recent developments in the world, the commission has decided to propose a new approach to the kyoto protocol. instead of taking real action to reduce the co2 and gas emissions responsible for the greenhouse effect and the climate change that results from it, the eu is making sure that 'flexible' mechanisms are used to not only avoid harm to industry but also to benefit it. this is despite the fact that the eu will not reach its target of reducing pollution by 8% by 2010. the principle of buying and selling everything has thus created a market for pollution rights! the directive is now being supplemented by 'joint implementing measures' and 'sustainable development mechanisms'. both concepts refer to industries that invest in cleaner technology in countries outside the eu. the reduction in pollution that is achieved is put on the assets side of the industries and they are given the right to pollute elsewhere (within the eu)! this is clearly another maneuver involving complicated and uncertain procedures to circumvent the substance of the kyoto protocol and to benefit companies, as the cost of implementing the protocol will be reduced by 20%. the report does not raise the slightest objection to the above measures and that is why the kke members of the european parliament voted against it.
in the case of the united kingdom, the commission has already taken the initiative in the form of a proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to carcinogens. the hypocrisy of this is shown by the fact that the persecution and banning of communists in many acceding countries and the annan plan for cyprus are considered to be in line with eu principles. in practice, the principles are so broad as to encompass everything that serves the interests of the monopolies, and so narrow as to exclude the fundamental rights of workers. the commission communication and the report by the committee on constitutional affairs deal with the question as a legal question and focus on the procedure for applying article 7 of the treaty, which provides for sanctions against member states which violate the principles of the union. the question is not only legal, however, it is deeply political. the report does not in any way call into question the 'good intentions' of the european union. however, it also contains positive elements such as the emphasis on the need to ensure a higher level of protection of fundamental rights and the acceptance or encouragement of a political climate in which people feel threatened, etc. for this reason, the kke members of the european parliament did not vote against the report, but neither for nor against it.
the rapporteur. mr president, i have to say that today's debate on the third cohesion report, on the fourth community support framework, is actually much easier than earlier debates we had on the same subject in the european parliament and i think that a key reason for this is commissioner barnier's personal contribution to this, in the sense that the dialogue on the new programming period 2007-2013 started very early and in this way it was possible to create, thanks to the help of the european parliament and thanks to having heard the views of the european parliament, a broad consensus on this very difficult issue. we must therefore give him credit and note, of course, that he was helped in this by the present commissioner, mrs fischler, and by mrs diamantopoulou. i should also add that this effort was even more difficult, given that we are now faced with enlargement, we have many new countries covered by the structural funds and the cohesion fund and there would have to be a fundamental reprogramming of the european union's regional policy. i think that the third cohesion report, as presented by the european commission, and the position of the european parliament clearly show the usefulness of this policy. and i think the first message that should be clear from this debate is that the european parliament, together with the european commission, is opposed to any thought of renationalizing this policy. the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of the environment in the event of a natural disaster is a good one, but it is not enough to say that the directive is a good one. i would also like to add that this policy, especially in view of enlargement, must be substantially supported by funding, and that is why parliament once again reiterates its position that its funding cannot be less than 0.46% of gross national product. we therefore agree with the european commission, which has in any case adopted a proposal from the european parliament on this subject. the european parliament welcomes the fact that the lion's share of the funding is going to what has hitherto been known as objective 1, the 'convergence objective' as it will be called from now on, and we believe that the problem created in the regions affected by statistics must be tackled effectively. they must be included in objective 1 and receive substantial financial support, and we also believe that these regions which are victims of statistics - and here we disagree with the european commission - should be included in the provisions of article 87 (3a) of the treaty in terms of state aid. the european parliament also believes, mr president, that the simplification of objective 2 is right, and we also believe that the initiatives we have known so far, equal, leader and urban, should be integrated into the basic programmes of the european union and remain the only community initiative, as a new objective 3, if you will, the interreg initiative for crossborder cooperation, which we believe adds to cooperation and development at european level. i would also like to say that our report gives particular attention to specific sectoral problems, such as the problems of rural areas, where we have received many proposals from the committee on agriculture and the committee on the environment. and as a member from greece, i want to focus on the problems arising from permanent geographical structural handicaps and they relate to areas such as islands, such as mountainous areas and, of course, the northern sparsely populated areas of finland and sweden. we believe that there should be specific policies not only in the context of regional policy, cohesion policy, but also in general in the other sectoral policies of the european union. we welcome the simplification of procedures which the european commission is attempting to make with the principle 'one fund, one programme', but we will wait to see the details and, mr president, we want the role of the european parliament, particularly in the preparation of the european commission's strategic documents for the new programming period, to be substantial and to have the right of co-decision of the european parliament. i would like to thank the commissioner for his reply.
the commission is not prepared to accept the amendments tabled by the committee on budgets. as you know, my colleague michel barnier has been appointed french foreign minister. he was therefore forced to leave the commission and, until his successor was appointed, president prodi entrusted me with the agendas of structural and regional policy. i therefore welcome the opportunity that the hatzidakis report gives us today to debate in principle the future of european economic, social and territorial cohesion policy. i would also like to thank you very much, mr hatzidakis, for your interesting report. the third report on economic and social cohesion was adopted by the commission on 18 february and presented to parliament by mr barnier on the same day. the commission's proposal is to be seen against the background of the proposal for the financial perspective, which we had also adopted earlier in february. the commission calls for a new partnership for cohesion. it must be our common desire to build not just a europe based on the common market, but a europe which is at the same time more competitive but also more solidary, a europe which will better enable us to achieve the lisbon and gothenburg objectives of sustainable development and a knowledge-based economy. the enlargement of the union on 1 may, which will bring many economically weak regions into the european union, has made a strong european policy of economic, social and territorial cohesion even more urgent and even more central. the commission has made a great effort to accommodate as far as possible the wishes of the various european institutions and regional and local authorities in its proposals for the future planning period 2007-13. the report is therefore not the mere idea of a single commissioner, but the result of a long and wide-ranging debate to which the european parliament has also made an important contribution. i refer in particular to the two important reports by mr mastorakis and mr pomés ruiz, which you adopted in september 2003 and to which mr hatzidakis also refers explicitly in his report. the debate, particularly at the level of finance ministers and heads of state, has only just begun on the question of the level of ambition we want to achieve with our cohesion policy. the report you have submitted today is a valuable contribution to an ambitious cohesion policy, as the commission is proposing, since the discussions on this subject will certainly be difficult. what specifically did we propose? firstly, a convergence objective for those regions whose gdp per capita is below 75% of the community average. secondly, the specific situation of the regions affected by the so-called statistical effect solely because of enlargement should be taken into account. thirdly, the cohesion fund should be integrated into these two actions. fourthly, a new objective to improve regional competitiveness and employment is to be introduced. fifthly, measures to promote interregional cooperation will be redesigned on the basis of the experience gained with interreg. in general, i can only agree with the rapporteur when he calls for a great deal of simplification in the future management of the structural funds. we have already made specific proposals for this, but we intend to put them into legal form in july. this applies to all areas, not just the area of responsibility of michel barnier. for example, we want to have a disconnect in the area for which i am responsible, rural development. we will be working to ensure that we first of all achieve the creation of a single rural development fund, which will bring together the various existing funds. secondly, there should be a single regulation setting out all the measures for rural development, and thirdly, the financial perspective should give priority to rural development. the same applies to the area of responsibility of former colleague mrs diamantopoulos and to the area previously held by mr michel barnier. we are moving here at a level where it is a question of developing a single instrument of programming and financing with corresponding common control rules. i think that would be a great step forward. ladies and gentlemen, i also agree with mr hatzidakis that 0.41% of community gdp - if you include rural development funds, it is 0.45% - should be regarded as a minimum for an effective cohesion policy. the report by mr hatzidakis also contains certain fears and concerns. the commission has already taken a number of initiatives in this area, including the difficult question of the definition of cohesion policy and the regional aid regime, which it has already raised. i am also personally concerned with the contribution of rural development to a balanced spatial planning policy and i can only assure you here today that we will continue to keep an eye on all the necessary measures and that, as i said, we are working to be able to present concrete proposals for regulations to parliament in the course of july.
the committee on economic and monetary affairs and industrial policy has adopted a number of amendments to the proposal for a council directive on the approximation of the laws of the member states relating to the permissible sound power level of towed vehicles. mr president, after congratulating the rapporteur warmly, i should like to stress on behalf of the committee on industry the importance of the contribution of industrial policy, and particularly research and innovation, to this third report on economic and social cohesion. it is clear that economic and social development, which is now geared towards the knowledge economy, requires overcoming the syndrome of peripherality and exploiting regional comparative advantage, innovation and research. to find their way, the regions must move away from the traditional path and focus on the growing economic importance of new industries, such as, for example, the ecological and cultural industries, which can offer new jobs but also overcome geographical constraints. economic and social cohesion requires, in addition to funding, new ideas, ingenuity and innovative methods.

commission. mr president, the basic regulation establishing the european monitoring centre for drugs and drug addiction has now been in place for ten years. it has been amended only on an ad hoc basis. the aim of the proposal was to reform the basic regulation on the centre in order to take into account the evolution in the patterns of drug use, the way in which the centre currently operates, the effects of enlargement and the horizontal measures that the commission would like to see applied to agencies in general. the legal basis of the centre remains unchanged: it is article 308. the main changes from the previous regulation are as follows. firstly, there is the strengthened role of the centre. the commission proposes that the centre should now be responsible for collecting, recording and analysing data on emerging trends in multiple drug use, including the combined use of legal and illegal psychotropic substances. in addition, the centre will devise tools and methods for evaluating drug policies and strategies implemented in the european union. the centre will also be able to provide technical assistance to the western balkans. secondly, in order to take account of enlargement, we have introduced a number of measures enabling the centre to operate effectively in an enlarged europe – for example the creation of a post of vice-chairman and a steering committee, and a review of the composition of the centre's scientific committee. given that the scientific committee is, by definition, a non-political body, we propose that it should be composed of only 18 experts appointed by the centre's management board on the basis of their qualifications. thirdly, there is the question of the participation of the european parliament in the management board. given that parliament is now the discharge authority for agencies, any potential conflict of interest that could arise should be avoided. i want to point out that out of the 15 community agencies only three – the environment agency, the medicines agency and the monitoring centre on racism and xenophobia – have european parliament representatives on their management boards. the other new element in the commission proposal is of a more technical nature, concerning either corrections related to the consolidation exercise or the updating of procedures in the new agencies. to conclude, this proposal introduces simplifications through consolidation and it should enable the european monitoring centre for drugs and drug addiction to operate effectively in an enlarged european union.
the committee on the environment, public health and consumer policy has adopted an opinion on the proposal for a council directive on the approximation of the laws of the member states relating to the protection of the health of workers from the risks related to exposure to asbestos at work. the commission has already taken a number of measures to ensure that the european union's drug policy is not distorted by the use of the newly created european monitoring centre for drugs and drug addiction. on the one hand, because of enlargement and, on the other, because of the new trends observed among young people in the parallel use of legal substances such as alcohol and illegal substances. the commission proposal establishes the main tasks of the centre as the collection, analysis and processing of transnational data on drug problems. i think it is necessary, however, that the centre should also move towards evaluating the different national policies rather than simply recording the data. only in this way will member states be provided with the knowledge of the best and most effective practices in this difficult field. mr president, i consider equally important the question of the representation of the european parliament on the centre's governing board, and i therefore propose that two members of parliament be appointed as representatives to report regularly to the committee responsible. the procedure for appointing the director should be the same as for all other community bodies, namely the submission of an application. the selection should be transparent and attractive to all potential candidates. i have tabled amendments to all these points and they have been accepted. in conclusion, i would like to thank the rapporteur for his constructive cooperation.

madam president, commissioner, ladies and gentlemen, the european union, despite all its successes, is still anxious to create new jobs, because this is one of the fundamental rights of european citizens, but it is also the best condition for our development and social cohesion. the employment guidelines and the luxembourg process in general are of great importance in achieving this objective, and that is why the committee on employment and social affairs is always examining them with particular interest, and our rapporteur, mrs hermange, has made a correct approach, for which we thank her and congratulate her. i would like to say that the commission has taken the initiative in this respect, and that it is in the interests of the community to ensure that the community's financial resources are used to the full. firstly, increasing employment is a complex process and policy. it covers all areas of economic policy, social policy, education policy, vocational training, transparency, the fight against tax evasion and undeclared work. research and innovation have been given a great deal of emphasis in recent times - it was a decision of the last spring council - and i thank the rapporteur for accepting an amendment of mine to that effect. this requires good coordination of all these policies in the design, implementation, monitoring and evaluation of the implementation of the guidelines. this policy of coordination is of great importance to the new member states, which are called upon to adopt european values in their internal policies. we have also seen that the countries where there is no good coordination are the ones that have the worst job growth. coordination and complementarity are required between european and national policies at all levels, central, regional, local, and between all the actors involved. i agree with the rapporteur's proposals for transparency and simplification of the procedure and information on the better use of the structural funds. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. it seems that this subject, however necessary it may be considered by all, is not easy. it requires political costs, social fermentation and maturity that we do not seem to have in the necessary degree. the consensus we rely on as a policy, to be meaningful and successful, requires well-informed and prepared citizens, responsible social partners and a serious effort to make the results of our policies visible and to build trust in the objectives and procedures we choose.
madam president, since this is the last debate on the broad guidelines of economic policy during the lifetime of this parliament, perhaps we could attempt a more general overview. the use of the term 'european economic policy' is certainly an abuse. the union has no original competence in this area, and unfortunately it does not have it under the new draft constitution. the original competence the single currency. the commission has already made a number of proposals to the council, and i am sure that the council will be able to accept them. the growth rate remained low, with correspondingly high unemployment. the atrophy of the real economy led to a crisis in public finances, large deficits in large economies, which resulted in the collapse due to non-implementation of the stability pact. the 25th of november 2003 should have freed us from the stability pact. but instead we are returning to the same tired discussions of how to revive it, instead of looking for the opportunities that could get us out of the wreckage of the european economy. there are, of course, two possibilities. the first is a genuine common european policy in the context of a mixed economy. i would like to ask the commissioner whether he is aware of the fact that the commission has not yet taken a decision on the matter. because if you take the 30 years after world war ii, the mixed economy achieved full employment and high growth rates. if you take the next 30 years, which the free-market doctrine triumphed over, we have a collapse of full employment, a collapse and weakening of the european economy. it is time to return to more reasonable principles, to a mixed economy such as the one that served us so well in the post-war years. we are told that unemployment is structural and to cure it we must take structural measures. that just means measures against the workers. i would reply to that that the lack of investment is structural and we must act against the speculation of the capital markets which has paralysed the investment mechanism. if we can find a new alliance of social forces, then we can indeed bring the european economy back to its old level. if we cannot find this alliance, then the european economy is doomed to wither and eventually become a satellite of the american economy. it is time we realised that.
in writing. - (el) the we voted against the report - not because we disagree, but because all the sensible measures are being cancelled out by the first amendment, which promotes coexistence between genetically modified, conventional and organic crops, allowing beekeepers to produce honey containing gmos. we consider this extremely dangerous and destructive to beekeeping, precisely because honey is a quality agricultural product. the presence of genetically modified crops, together with the 0,9% gmos limit in honey, is based on the assumption that bees are able to choose between genetically modified crops and non-genetically modified crops. we condemn the european parliament's efforts to increase the coexistence of genetically modified, conventional and organic crops - despite the warning bells ringing from several well-founded scientific studies - and we would stress that no legislative measure can prevent or combat the enormous potential dangers. the argument that the cultivation and movement of gmos is now a done deal and that all that is missing is 'satisfactory' legislation is hypocritical, as what is presented as a fait accompli would not exist if the eu had not yielded to pressure from the us. we are categorically against the cultivation and movement of gmos and we will continue to fight for a complete ban on them, because we do not believe that anything is settled. the european parliament should take the same position. but parliament seems to have given in to pressure and other interests too long ago.
(parliament adopted the legislative resolution) the aim of europass is not to help workers but to make it easier for employers to compare jobseekers and select those who will be most profitable with the lowest requirements, regardless of their country of origin. in the context of the 'free' movement of workers who will have to wander from country to country as nomads in search of work, this measure contributes to depressing wages and demands by using workers from poorer countries as a lever. at the same time, europass makes large companies responsible for assessing the educational and vocational qualifications of workers, thus removing this role from the education system. it helps to decouple qualifications from the right to work. several types of training and informal training are given priority over qualifications acquired through systematic training. in this way, europass becomes yet another tool which leads to the replacement of comprehensive, integrated training with 'flexible' sporadic training and incomplete skills to meet market requirements. there is no doubt that information on the so-called social competence of workers, that is to say, their behaviour at work and their acceptance of all the requirements that employers may place on them, will also soon be required. for these reasons, members of the communist party of greece in parliament voted against the report.
the commission's proposal for a directive on the protection of workers from the risks related to exposure to carcinogens and mutagens is a very good one. in the name of competitiveness and productivity, the aim is to reduce wage and non-wage costs, thereby increasing exploitation and bypassing social security entitlements. great caution, freezing/reduction of wages/pensions and new privileges for companies are introduced in order to ensure the return of big capital. the eu's employment policy aims to replace full-time and fixed-term work with part-time and temporary work, while ignoring basic employment, wage and social security rights under the misleading lisbon headline of 'sustainable development and full employment'. the eight-hour day and collective agreements are under attack, and the hiring out of workers, a form of slave trade, unacceptably low wages and increasing profits for the plutocracy are being introduced. the millions of unemployed are now being joined by millions of underemployed. these policies have not solved and cannot solve the problem of unemployment, nor do they establish the rights to guarantee the right to full-time work and to stable employment, because the real causes lie in the mechanisms by which capitalism is governed. we support the workers' struggle to abolish these policies and demand a different, grassroots-oriented policy with more social rights and higher wages in line with modern demands.
in the case of the commission, the commission's proposal for a regulation on the financial envelope for the enlarged union 2007-2013 was rejected because it did not meet the criteria for the enlarged union. these objectives are an expression of the fundamental choices of big business: the promotion of the anti-working-class projections and schemes of the lisbon process under the pretext of combating unemployment, the support for and massive expansion of a supranational and uncontrolled mechanism for the persecution of grassroots movements under the pretext of security and the strengthening and militarization of the eu under the pretext of combating terrorism. we condemn the efforts to limit the resources available for social and regional cohesion, however false these objectives may have proved to be, and to increase the resources for plans to wage war against and persecute the peoples of the eu and third countries. we are against the principle of national responsibility for the consequences of policies decided at community level being reflected in future eu funds, and we are against the people having to pay the price twice. the solution to the problems of the people of the member states of the european union is not a matter of a few dollars and cents, but the struggle of the workers for a radical change in the policies pursued by their governments in the european union, the struggle for the elections of the grassroots to prevail over those of big business.
(parliament adopted the legislative resolution) the report shows once again that the eu's regional policy has failed. there are still huge disparities in the development of several regions, while the outlook for the poorer member states is unfavourable. the purely statistical approach risks excluding many areas of the present member states from funding with enlargement. in greece, for example, four areas will not be financed under the fourth community support framework, while three others are at the border. these areas cover 75% of the population of the country and so far most of them have qualified as objective 1 areas with severe and growing social and economic problems which the proposed temporary transitional support will only exacerbate. greece is a typical example for another reason, namely the promotion and concentration of resources in certain areas, such as attica, which exacerbates the wider regional problem of urban overpopulation. at the same time, despite all the talk about support for remote areas, the eu continues to unjustifiably - and despite our repeated proposals - to exclude the aegean islands from special treatment for remote areas, despite their serious problems. "cohesion" and "rising levels of development" are in practice in contradiction with the policies that preach them, because these policies serve big capital. in practice, it can only create deviation, marginalization, unemployment and underdevelopment.
